**Sequencing and ownership (what Plans 5–8b keep, and the Plan 4 stub for each):**
- **R4-01 Plan 3 gate.** Ruling: Plan 4 runs on feat/p4-regions rebased onto merged feat/p3-holds; Task 5 first checks that every Plan 2 and Plan 3 name this contract cites exists with the cited signature (a divergence stops the task for a controller ruling); Tasks 1–4, 6 and 7 (`src/regions/**`, `test/fixtures/regions/**`, `src/entry/worker.ts`, `tools/build/build.mjs`, Plan 2's `src/data/attrs.ts` (one attribute, R4-20), devDependencies, appended lint rows) may start once Plan 2 has merged and are rebased onto merged Plan 3 before Task 5; Plan 3's round-2 items (rev 2.5 QUEUE Q5) are Plan 3's tasks (reconciliation 2026-10-08): the `last_check` store and its writes are Plan 3 Task 10's (X-05, X-06) — Task 10 here only returns the failed entries in the verdict — and the F10 kernel `src/holds/revertCs.ts` is Plan 3 Task 17's (X-09), whose `revertInverse()` results reach `predictRevert` (Task 12) through Plan 7's revert verb calling `Engine.predictRevert` — the roadmap orders 3 → 4 and only the claim-free region kernel is independent of the holds — cost if wrong: idle time for the early tasks and rebase conflicts in `package.json` and `tools/lint/rules.ts` only. (3X-06, 3Q-03)
- **R4-02 Import order.** Ruling: `src/regions` sits after `src/data` and before `src/board`, `src/claims` after `src/holds` and before `src/engine`: the regions reuse `rows`, `stripEnding`, `isBinaryBytes`, `conflictMarkerRows`, `AttrSnapshot` and `PathAttrs` (never copied, Plan 3 "Shared helpers"), the fold can import the pure `foldOverlap` and mapper, Plan 3's `mask.ts` can import the mapper, and `src/claims` can implement Plan 3's ports and read `FoldState.claims`; `src/data/conflict.ts` reaches region code only through `ConflictCtx` callbacks bound by the engine (the `concatAllowed` pattern); no `src/regions` module imports `src/board`, `src/holds`, `src/claims` or `src/engine`, so `touch` and `gate` cannot read the hold registry (REG-13, HLD-06); the reconciliation's one cross-plan order (X-110) keeps these two slots and adds `src/text`, `src/i18n` (before `src/git`), `src/since` (between `src/board` and `src/holds`) and `src/claude` → `src/coord` → `src/lifecycle` → `src/cli` after `src/engine` (header "Architecture"); an earlier plan's module never imports a later plan's, and a later plan implements an earlier plan's port in its own file (X-110, X-111) — 04 §3.1 ("a library loaded by the daemon"), roadmap ("disjoint modules (`src/regions/**` …)"), Plan 2 "each importing only from the directories listed before it" — cost if wrong: a moved module. (3Q-07, 3X-02)
- **R4-03 K-kind ownership.** Ruling: Plan 4 owns the detection verdicts of every region-based kind and creates records only where Plan 3 already provides the constructor and Plan 4 owns the call site: K3 (B1 block of a seat CS, a `claimPrecheck` failure, an own claim op folded invalid by overlap) and K6 (B1 block of a human CS) become Plan 3 PINs with guard `claim:<claim_id>` made by Plan 3's B1 slot; K4 by placement (an identity or fence failure routed to `conflict()` → `TAKE_TIP`); the D8 step-4 tie-break adds `claim-breach` to the K4 record (K5 on the record side); `breach` hits (K5 input) go to `BreachPort` (a log sink until Plan 6); K7 items 1–2 are `T0RegionsPort` verdicts (Plan 3 makes the PIN); K1 and K2 are `predict`/`confirm` verdicts only — SHADOW and K2 PIN creation are Plan 5's hook flows; every `event` op, request, notice and text is Plan 6's — glossary §10 "Detected by", Plan 2 out-list ("K1/K2/K3/K5/K6/K7"), roadmap row 4 ("K3/K4/K5/K7 detection"), R3-07, R3-08 — cost if wrong: a constructor call moves to Plan 5. (OQ-36, 2Q-19, 3Q-04)
- **R4-04 Seats before Plan 5.** Ruling: Plan 4 adds no production seat source: `Engine.seatEffect(e)` (CONFIRM and Algorithm CLAIM for one confirmed tool effect) is production code whose callers before Plan 5 are tests and the SIM's scripted seats, which also queue `seat_start`/`seat_end` drafts through `core.outbox.add` (PRO-20: in the same or an earlier board commit than the seat's first claim) and send Plan 3 `CsSignal`s through `Engine.signal`; production builds before Plan 5 therefore fold and gate against no claims (B1 never blocks, `claims_held` = 0), while placement, the tripwires, T0 items 1–2, the edit check and the symbol delta change production behaviour from the moment Task 16 binds them — PRO-17 needs a live seat; Plan 2's out-list gives `seat_start` emission to Plan 5; R3-05 is the precedent — cost if wrong: a seat-shaped defect surfaces in Plan 5's FakeHost SIM. (2Q-17, 3Q-05, OQ-19)
- **R4-05 Attribution before Plan 5.** Ruling: `gate` reads the seat set `S(h)` of each hunk from `HunkAttributionPort.seatsOf(csIds, path, hunk, self)`; `csAttribution` (the binding in v1 — verify pass V-8: Plan 5's CCI-27 windows already decide which CS a delta lands in, so the cs_id grammar carries CCI-27's answer at path granularity; Plan 5 adds no per-hunk binding) answers the seats of the group's CSs whose paths include p (`<seat_id>/<turn-seq>` → that seat; `<peer_id>/human/<n>` and a recovered CS → the peer's `human`), so a hunk of a multi-CS group is attributed to every member that touched p — REG-19 "Attribution is CCI-27's table", HLD-45 seat-level foreignness — cost if wrong: an over-attributed hunk blocks a CS that only shares the path (the safe direction). (OQ-18, 2Q-06, 2X-12)
- **R4-06 Grants and reservations before Plan 6.** Ruling: Plan 4 implements the fold shapes (`claims[*].grant`, `reserved_for_req`), B1 admission of a touching hunk only for the grant's `cs_id` of the grantee seat, the holder's own hunks never blocked by its granted claim, `predict.ownTouches[].granted`, the grant ends (a) landing trailer in fold step 4, (c) grantee seat end, leave, supersede, (d) claim release, and the pure helper `reservationValid`; `request`, `request_status`, `answer` and `presume_absent` keep folding as Plan 2 no-ops until Plan 6, which calls `applyGrant`, `endGrants`, `endGrant`, `reservationValid`, `staleClaims`, `releaseClaimsByAnswer` and `releaseStaleClaims` from their effects (the last three added by Task 8 for Plan 6, X-25; Plan 6's `CommitCtx` builds `reservationValid`'s `earlierInCommit`) — so before Plan 6 no grant exists in production and every claim op carrying `reserved_for_req` folds invalid (its batch has no folded `answer{release}`); tests use hand-built fold states — PRO-19, 02 §3.6, Plan 2 R-12 ("Plans 3, 4 and 6 add their effects") — cost if wrong: Plan 6 moves a helper. (OQ-21, 2Q-04, 3Q-06, 2X-16)
- **R4-07 Base moves, save release, EPOCH_BASE change.** Ruling: Plan 7 (R-08) produces base-move commits, `placementCheck(tree(B), tree(TIP), tree(Hr), newtree)` with ENG-48, `save` and its PRO-32 release decision, and the EPOCH_BASE change that takes a claimed path out of `LIVE` (REG-32); Plan 4 implements the verdicts they need — `breach(...)[].baseMove`, `placementCheck` sub-check (a) callable on any four trees, `saveReleaseDue(...)` for PRO-32 (a)–(b) as a pure predicate, the PRO-23 `save` fold effect and EMIT's "never re-anchored onto a non-LIVE path" — and tests them on hand-built commits (`commitTree` with a `Cowork-Base` trailer and two parents; a folded `save` op) — Plan 2 R-08 — cost if wrong: none; the predicates are pure. (2Q-22, 3Q-16)
- **R4-08 Owner decisions of 2026-10-08.** Ruling: **D1** (final, glossary §29.1, §29.8) — the edit check's failed list is display data shown in full whenever `/cowork status --flow` or `/cowork wrap` runs, in the terminal and in slash-command output, never pushed unasked: Plan 4 returns every failed entry, sorted by `(check ?? 9, path, line ?? 0, reason)`, with only `check?`, `path`, `reason` (a closed enum, R4-42) and `line?` — no label, symbol or code text, because slash output enters the seat's Claude context (SEC-38) — writes it nowhere and adds no `tty` filter; there is no gate on who asked (X-01: the daemon puts `last_check` into every all-paths `flow{detail: 'flow'}` reply and every `wrap` reply, whoever asked, and `null` into a path-scoped or `detail: 'section'` one); rev 2.5 already carries the final D1 text, so this plan proposes no D1 spec edit (X-03; spec edit 1 dropped); the reply composition is Plan 5's (X-01), the rendering Plan 7's, the store and its writes Plan 3's (`ChangeSets.lastCheck`, X-05, X-06). **D2** — a confirmed F10 revert is an intended shrink for the blob it wrote only: the preview runs the edit check in mode `predict`, which drops check 4's size part (`shrink`, `emptied`, `deleted`) and keeps its label-count part; checkpoints keep both parts with no exemption; T0 items 1–2 have no shrink clause. **D3, D5** — no effect. **D4** — the bundled `web-tree-sitter` and grammar licences and their `SHA256SUMS` lines are inputs to Plan 7's preparation (licence, scrub list) and to the release task that ends Plan 8b (X-102: Plan 7 Task 19 writes `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` for `web-tree-sitter` and the five grammars, checked by L-DIST, and 8b's stage C1 verifies it; X-70: Plan 7 Task 19 writes `dist/SHA256SUMS`); Plan 4 publishes nothing — the binding owner decisions; glossary §29.1 D1's own alternative ("If the owner wants slash display, the one change is …") — cost if wrong: a display rule moves; no verdict changes. (OQ-28, 2Q-21, R2-K05, R2-K06, D-01…D-03)

**Runtime, workers and budgets:**
- **R4-09 The runtime dependency exception.** Ruling: `web-tree-sitter` (exact 0.27.x) and `tree-sitter-python`, `tree-sitter-javascript`, `tree-sitter-typescript`, `tree-sitter-c-sharp` (exact versions) are devDependencies pinned in `package-lock.json`; esbuild bundles `web-tree-sitter` into `dist/worker.js` only; `tools/build/build.mjs` copies `tree-sitter.wasm` and the five grammar files (`tree-sitter-python.wasm`, `tree-sitter-javascript.wasm`, `tree-sitter-typescript.wasm`, `tree-sitter-tsx.wasm`, `tree-sitter-c_sharp.wasm`) into `dist/grammars/`; lint L-REGION-DEPS allows that one import only under `src/regions/worker/**`; a bundle test asserts no other `dist/*.js` contains it — 04 §1.1, §3.3 "Runtime", glossary §6 (`worker.js`, `grammars/*.wasm`); Plan 1's constraint is read as "no install-time dependency" — cost if wrong: Plan 7's packaging moves file locations. (OQ-1, 3Q-01, 3X-01)
- **R4-10 Pinned versions and the ABI spike.** Ruling: Task 1's first step installs web-tree-sitter 0.27.0 with tree-sitter-python 0.25.0, tree-sitter-javascript 0.25.0, tree-sitter-c-sharp 0.23.5 and the newest tree-sitter-typescript whose `.wasm` loads (the research versions), loads every grammar in a worker and parses one fixture per language; any load or ABI failure stops the task for a controller ruling (another 0.27.x patch or grammar version, never a second parser, REG-03); the versions are recorded once in `src/regions/runtime.ts` (`GRAMMAR_VERSIONS`) and a unit test checks them against `package-lock.json` — 04 §3.3 "(pinned versions, `.wasm`)"; research runtime-dist.md ("UNKNOWN: whether the c-sharp grammar's ABI works with web-tree-sitter 0.27") — cost if wrong: a stalled Task 1, found first. (OQ-3)
- **R4-11 Query files, the runtime `.wasm` and the `regionSpec` parts.** Ruling: cowork's query files are TypeScript string constants, one module per language under `src/regions/queries/`, compiled into `dist/worker.js` and imported by the main thread only to hash them; `regionSpec = regionSpecId([...sha256 hex of each dist/grammars/*.wasm file (tree-sitter.wasm included), ...sha256 hex of each query source's UTF-8 bytes, 'extent-convention/1', 'label-convention/1', 'FoldMapper/1'])` with Plan 2's `regionSpecId` (sorted, joined by `'\n'`); a part is the bare lowercase digest (no file name); `loadRegionRuntime` computes it from the shipped files (a grammar that later fails to load in a worker does not change it, REG-03's grammarless fallback is per peer and P2-safe); a golden test pins the value for the pinned files; spec edit: glossary §6's `grammars/*.wasm` row names `tree-sitter.wasm` and says the query files ship inside `worker.js` — 04 §2 "regionSpec", REG-04, Plan 2 `regionSpecId` — cost if wrong: one regionSpec change across a team (the designed VERSION path). (OQ-2, OQ-7, 3Q-09, 2X-25)
- **R4-12 Worker file access.** Ruling: `loadRegionRuntime(fs, dir)` reads the `.wasm` files on the main thread through the `Fs` port; `threadWorkers` posts the bytes to each worker at spawn (`Language.load(Uint8Array)`, the runtime initialised from bytes); worker modules import no `fs`, `child_process`, `src/git` or `src/ports/fs.ts` (L-REGION-DEPS) — 11 L-PORTS "`fs` use in the region module (no writes, REG-)", REG-02, Plan 2 L-FS-PORT — cost if wrong: ≈ 7 MB copied per worker at spawn (measured once in Task 1). (OQ-4)
- **R4-13 Pool, priorities and SIM determinism.** Ruling: `RegionWorkers` is a port; production binds `threadWorkers(runtime, {threads: 2, workerPath, clock})` — worker `fg` serves `predict`/`confirm` re-parses, `gate`, T0, placement and the edit check, worker `bg` serves label verification, `foldOverlap` batches, draft post-image tables and save attribution, with one priority queue `fg > save > bg` and `bg`-class jobs allowed on `fg` when it is idle; unit tests and SIM bind `inlineWorkers(runtime)` (the same job handlers on the calling thread, FIFO); budgets are `Deadline`s from `RegionBudgets` on the Clock port, so in SIM (virtual clock, inline jobs) no budget expires unless a test injects the overrun through `BudgetInjector` (test builds only) — 04 §3.1, §3.18 ("Event-loop block ≤ 20 ms"), 08 §3.7.2 ("save's own priority in the REG worker pool"), TST-03 — cost if wrong: production timing differs from SIM, covered by T-REG-15 and T-REG-29 on real time (Task 18). (OQ-5, 3Q-11, 3X-03)
- **R4-14 Caches.** Ruling: tables, line arrays and label counts live on the main thread in one `TableCache` (300 MB LRU by blobRef, size = bytes of the line array plus a per-unit estimate); a `DiskSnapshotId` that a draft references as `srcBlob` is pinned (its line array and table never evicted; unpinned entries go first above 300 MB); the cache also indexes tables by path in recency order for REG-18's last-good table; parse trees live only in workers (≤ 8 per worker, LRU by blobRef) for incremental re-parse; a cache hit equals a recompute (TC-REG-10 (b)) — 04 §3.3 "Cache", "Last-good table", §3.18 — cost if wrong: memory, bounded by the cap. (OQ-6)
- **R4-15 Deadlines.** Ruling: a job carries its deadline into the worker, which checks it between files, between query captures and through the parser's progress callback, and returns a typed `deadline` result; the main thread abandons a job at its deadline (the caller gets `NO_TABLE{budget}`, `WHOLE_FILE` in `local` mode or `incomplete`) and terminates and respawns a worker that has not answered 1 s after the deadline (04 §6 "worker crashes": `NO_TABLE{budget}`, `REGION_BUDGET` once per path per 30 min, never for the edit check); the terminate/respawn applies to the region pool only — Plan 7's secret-scan pool is a separate pool instance on the same `src/entry/worker.ts` entry, which dispatches by job kind, and Plan 7 never terminates region workers (X-37) — 04 §3.18, §3.20 ("the job is abandoned"), REG-31 — cost if wrong: a respawn (a wasm reload) after a rare stall. (R2-C02, R2-H01)
- **R4-16 Extensions.** Ruling: the language comes from the byte-exact lowercase extension of the path's last component (`.py .pyi` → python, `.js .mjs .cjs .jsx` → javascript, `.ts .mts .cts` → typescript, `.tsx` → tsx, `.cs` → c_sharp); `Foo.PY` has no grammar (classes 5/6) — 04 §3.3 lists lowercase only; coarser on doubt (P2) — cost if wrong: an upper-case file gets block claims. (OQ-27)

**Coordinates, the mapper and tables:**
- **R4-17 FoldMapper/1 is new code and the one line mapper, held ranges included.** Ruling: `src/regions/mapper.ts` implements 04 §3.4 exactly (its own line equality, prefix/suffix trimming, Myers O(ND) with the greedy forward snake and leftmost tie-break, the image rules, D > 5,000 → `WHOLE_FILE` in both modes, the 200 ms cap in `local` mode only); Plan 2's `matchedRows` stays 03-data's canon helper and is never imported by `src/regions`, `src/claims` or a region consumer (L-ONE-MAPPER); Task 5 re-bases Plan 3's held ranges (HLD-21, `src/holds/mask.ts`) on `hunks()` — the only change is the row source, one import — because 04 P3 and §3.1 name `hunks` for the ±1-line held region and Plan 5's G1 compares held rows with `predict`'s FoldMapper/1 rows (the same-tail insertion lands after line 5341 in both); a Plan 3 held-range test that changes its expected value stops the task for a controller ruling — REG-07, T-REG-20 — cost if wrong: one import reverted. (OQ-32, 2Q-15, 3Q-13, B3-08)
- **R4-18 Blank rows, blank-line blocks and gap blocks.** Ruling: a row is blank iff, after removing one trailing `\r` (and a line-1 BOM), it is empty or only bytes `0x20`/`0x09` (a byte test, valid for non-UTF-8 blobs); a blank-line block (class 6) and a gap block are maximal runs of non-blank rows (a gap block also bounded by unit extents and `<imports>`), so blank rows are left out of exactly those two kinds of block; every other unit keeps the blank rows inside its extent — a function or member is one contiguous `[start, end]` range with its internal blank rows, and a class/type residue is its extent minus the extents of its member leaf units, blank rows between members included (extent-convention/1 step 4, multi-range); a blank row outside every extent (between top-level units) belongs to no unit; "blank-line-only hunks claim nothing" is a rule of Algorithm CLAIM's unit choice (Task 11), not of the unit shape, so a foreign hunk that turns a blank residue row into code is a base-overlap touch of the residue claim — 04 §1.1, §2 "gap block" and "residue", §3.3 extent-convention/1 step 4, §3.13 ("Blank-line-only hunks claim nothing") — cost if wrong: one block boundary moves. (OQ-15)
- **R4-19 Labels of non-symbol units.** Ruling: block units (class-6 blocks and gap blocks) carry `{kind: 'block', qualname: '', simple: '', type: '', disc: ''}`; file claims `{kind: 'file', …}` with empty fields; a module-level `NAME = …` is `{kind: 'const', qualname: <dotted enclosing names>.NAME, simple: 'NAME'}`; residue keeps its class/type's label; C# operators, conversion operators, indexers and destructors use CLR metadata names in `simple` (`op_Addition`, `op_Implicit`, `Item`, `Finalize`), constructors the type's simple name with `kind: 'ctor'`, so no label field contains whitespace; a label key is `kind + '\0' + qualname + '\0' + disc` — glossary §7 requires `label`; SEC-35 applies `validLabel` per non-empty field; T-F12-draft "whole-file label" — cost if wrong: a display string. (OQ-16, OQ-31)
- **R4-20 `working-tree-encoding`.** Ruling: Task 4 extends Plan 2's `AttrCache` (`check-attr` adds `working-tree-encoding`) with `PathAttrs.workingTreeEncoding: string | null`; any value puts the path in class 3 — 04 §3.3 class 3, §1.1 b4 — cost if wrong: one attribute name. (OQ-14)
- **R4-21 Region fixtures are regenerated.** Ruling: the research corpus (`rc_touch`, `results_all.json` S1–S10, `fwd`, `premerge`, `big_wt.py`/`big_lf.py`, `rc_valid_errors`, `rc_dupes`, X7, x2/x3, S7, S9, the 26,436-line and 1.3 MB files, `hold/exp_a`/`exp_bc`) is not in the repository; Task 2 regenerates each as a checked-in fixture or a seeded generator under `test/fixtures/regions/` with a provenance README naming the 04/05 sentence each reproduces; every VERIFIED number of 04 is an expected value (432 units on 5,960 lines, `compute_total`'s post-image extent 5331–5340, the same-tail insertion after line 5341 where git places it after 5339, the 15 rc_touch R2 verdicts, the `merge-tree` same-tail misplacement); a value the spec does not give (most T-REG-20 golden hunks) is pinned at the first green run and reviewed by the controller — 04 §7 harness, `docs/research/consolidation/cat_regions.md` (citations only) — cost if wrong: a fixture differs from the lost original where the spec pins nothing. (OQ-24, 2Q-07, 3Q-15)
- **R4-22 Two test-spec inconsistencies.** Ruling: TC-REG-10 (a) compares verdicts among forms of one path class (BOM+CRLF vs LF for grammar paths; cp949 vs cp949 with CRLF; UTF-16LE vs UTF-16LE with BOM) and a separate case pins the grammarless verdict of cp949 and the binary verdict of UTF-16LE; 11 §8 Q7's "a region mapper in WHOLE_FILE mode counts as a fail" is read as: a `WHOLE_FILE` image only makes check 1's `line` fall back to the first span of `M[p]`, the verdict is unchanged — both listed as 11 spec edits — 04 §3.3 classes 2, 5, 6; §3.20 "Failed entries"; REG-36 ("`line` MUST NOT change the verdict") — cost if wrong: one property reworded. (OQ-25, 2Q-11)
- **R4-23 A spec typo.** Ruling: 04 §3.4 step 3's "boundary insertions are decided by `touch` case 3" means `touch` step 4; listed as a spec edit — §3.6 step 4 is the `k == hi`/`k == lo−1` case — none. (OQ-33)

**Claims and the fold:**
- **R4-24 `claim_id` and validators.** Ruling: `newClaimId(r)` = RFC 4648 base32, lowercase, unpadded, of 10 bytes from the `Random` port (16 characters, `^[a-z2-7]{16}$`), not Plan 2's Crockford `base32c`; Plan 2's `IdKind` gains `'claim'` and `src/sec/validators.ts` gains `validLabel(field)` (SEC-35's pattern `^[\p{L}\p{M}\p{N}_.:#<>,$@\[\]()-]{1,200}$` per non-empty field); a claim op whose `claim_id` fails `validId('claim')` is a fold no-op, a label field failing `validLabel` still folds and renders `?` — glossary §3 ("16 lowercase base32 chars from 80 random bits"), SEC-35, Plan 3's test value `claim:abcdefghij234567` — cost if wrong: an alphabet change before any board carries claims. (OQ-13, 2Q-10, 3Q-19, 2X-10)
- **R4-25 Typed ops.** Ruling: `src/board/ops.ts` declares `ClaimOp` and `ReleaseOp` with glossary §7's fields exactly (as R3-24 did for `hold`): `OpaqueOp.type` loses `'claim' | 'release'`, `PLAN2_FOLDED_TYPES` gains both (name kept); `parseOpLine` validates field by field (`validId('claim')`; `validPathSyntax`; `anchor` an oid; `base_blob` an oid, or `null` only in the absent-path form with `ranges: []` and ≥ 1 tombstone; ranges 1-based inclusive, `lo ≤ hi`, sorted and disjoint; `granularity` enum; `label` five strings; `cs_id` `validId('cs')`; `holder {peer: hex128, seat, agent}`; `reserved_for_req` a ULID when present; `release.reason` never `superseded`); unknown fields are ignored — glossary §7, REG-08, PRO-05, SEC-35 — cost if wrong: none. (B2-01, R2-L02, R2-L05)
- **R4-26 The fold with claims stays pure.** Ruling: `claimFoldNeeds(prev, c)` (pure, `src/board/fold.ts`) lists, for one board commit, every tree entry its `claim` ops need (`(c.liveCode, path)` for `base_blob`; each tombstone anchor's `(anchor, path)` for SEC-35's tree part and for mapping) and every overlap pair the fold may ask (each `claim` op against every active claim of a different holder seat on its path in `prev` and against every earlier `claim` op of the same commit on that path — a superset of the pairs the fold decides); `Folder.foldTo` resolves them through an injected `ClaimInputsResolver` (`src/claims/foldInputs.ts`: tree entries by `GitRepo.readTreeMap`, line arrays by one `readBlobs` per commit, `foldOverlap` answers by one `foldOverlapBatch` worker job per commit — FoldMapper/1 in `fold` mode, no time cap; the pure step from the named needs and the resolved tree entries to the `OverlapClaim` pairs, the tombstone ancestry rule included, is `overlapPairsOf` of the import-light module `src/claims/overlapPairs.ts`, which the viewer shares, R4-27) and passes `ClaimFoldInputs` as `foldCommit`'s optional fourth argument; `foldCommit` stays pure, total and synchronous; with the argument absent every claim effect is skipped exactly as in Plan 2 (`claim` and `release` verdict `unfolded`; `join`, `leave`, `seat_end` and `save` leave `claims` untouched), and `Folder` always passes it (a test-build assertion fails any `unfolded` claim verdict when inputs were passed); a missing object makes the resolver throw `FoldObjectMissingError {oid}` — the fold does not advance past the previous commit, and `prepareIteration` catches it and ends the iteration with `{ok: false, why: 'fold_object_missing', oid}` (no apply, no publish; R4-76) so the next fetch retries (a miss that persists is a `not_converged` cause `fold_object_missing`, `degraded` after 60 s, with no LOST_STATE escalation in v1 — X-31; the escalation question is the 02 owner's); T-PRO-11's "≤ 50 ms for ≤ 1,000 ops (excluding mapper time)" times `foldCommit` alone — 02 §3.3 inputs ("blobs reachable from live code commits, read only through the 04-regions mapper"), 04 §3.9 step 2, §6 ("the fold does not guess"), REG-31, Plan 2 ("Pure, total, never throws"), INV-REG-FOLD (the answers are a deterministic function of op fields and blobs, so a peer whose mapper is 10× slower folds identically) — cost if wrong: one prefetch per board commit that carries claim ops. (OQ-11, 2Q-05, 3Q-02, A-11, 2X-09)
- **R4-27 The viewer's fold (decided by the owner on 2026-10-08: X-92's alternative, real-time claims, OWNER-CHOICES item 1).** Ruling: the viewer's own fold takes claim inputs exactly as `Folder` does — on every board commit (with the argument absent R4-26 skips every claim effect, the claim effects of `join`, `leave`, `seat_end` and `save` included, so a commit without claim ops gets inputs too; its needs are empty and it costs no read): it computes `claimFoldNeeds(prev, c)`, reads the named tree entries and blobs through its own read-only reader (8a Task 20's `readClaimInputs`: one `cat-file --batch` per claim-carrying commit, `merge-base --is-ancestor` for the tombstone rule), pairs them with this plan's `overlapPairsOf` and answers each pair with `foldOverlap` in the viewer process, so its claim verdicts are `effective` or `rejected` and its state equals the daemons' checkpoints; the blob bytes stay in memory for that one fold step and are never written, logged, cached across folds, served, rendered or counted (12 §3.3.3, VIZ-06, INV-VIZ-NOCONTENT as narrowed on 2026-10-08); there is no checkpoint re-seed and no claims-only mismatch suppression — a checkpoint mismatch is a defect, shown as VIZ-09 says; a missing object never makes the viewer fold that commit without inputs (that would diverge from the daemons for good): its room keeps its last folded scene with `UI_VIZ_CLAIMS_PENDING` and retries until this PC's daemon has fetched the object, as `Folder` waits for it (the viewer never fetches). Plan 4's part is `overlapPairsOf` with its parity tests (Task 8) and the claim rows (follow-up 4-V, R4-68) — 12 §3.3.2 ("Same code, same build"), §3.3.3, VIZ-06, 02 §3.3 inputs, OWNER-CHOICES item 1 — cost if wrong: the viewer goes back to claims at checkpoints only (8a Task 20 and 4-V reverted, the narrowed invariant restored). (V-03, 3Q-02)
- **R4-28 Own claim-op rejections.** Ruling: Plan 2's `OwnRejectionReason` gains `'claim_overlap' | 'claim_invalid'` and `OwnRejection` gains three optional fields set only on claim rejections — `claimId?`, `overlaps?` and `claimReason?` — so every non-claim rejection keeps exactly Plan 2's and Plan 3's shape and their deep-equal assertions stay unchanged; `Folder.takeClaimRejections()` drains them (Plan 3's `takeHoldRejections` pattern); a generic-check rejection keeps Plan 2's renumbering and is never K3 (PRO-24 bullet 1); `claim_overlap` marks the covering CS blocked by `overlaps` in the claim store, so its group's next B1 call returns that block and Plan 3's slot makes the K3 PIN (PRO-24 bullet 2, HLD-45); `claim_invalid` (anchor, `base_blob`, ranges, a SEC-35 validator, a reservation without its answer) leaves the claim TENTATIVE and local, never re-published automatically, and raises `CLAIM_REJECTED {claim_id, reason}` once per `(claim_id, reason)` (PRO-24 bullet 3, PRO-38) — 02 §3.5 row "its `claim` op folds invalid (overlap …)" — cost if wrong: a reason value. (B2-04, R2-L09, A-04)
- **R4-29 Claim order without a fold-state field.** Ruling: 02 §3.3's `claims` record has no board position, so `Folder` keeps a daemon-local `claimOrder` index (claim_id → height and line of the op that last made the record HELD; a re-anchor keeps it); claims already present in a starting checkpoint get that checkpoint's height with ties broken by `claim_id`; folded claims precede every TENTATIVE claim, TENTATIVE claims follow the local confirmation sequence; the index feeds only local decisions (REG-21's sibling order, the D8 step-4 tie-break, the earliest claim of a B1 block, display) — never a fold or cross-peer verdict, because the fold never lets two overlapping claims of different seats both be HELD (INV-REG-NOVALID-OVERLAP) — 04 §2 "claim order"; 02 §3.3's state shape is verbatim and read by Plan 8a — cost if wrong: two peers that started from different checkpoints pick a different "earliest" claim for a display or a guard name; flagged for 02. (derived)
- **R4-30 Record-creating claim ops.** Ruling: a `claim` op is record-creating (PRO-06) iff its `claim_id` has no record in the folded state at intent build (a re-claim of a compacted id included); Plan 3's outbox withhold predicate is widened to it, so at the 5,000-record cap new claims stay TENTATIVE and `BOARD_FULL` is raised once per episode (Plan 3's episode), while re-anchors, re-claims of a non-compacted RELEASED/STALE record and every `release` keep flowing — PRO-06, PRO-17 ("the record cap is not reached for a new id"), 02 §6 ("new claims stay TENTATIVE") — cost if wrong: none. (B3-07, R2-L18)
- **R4-31 Drafts are memory-only.** Ruling: confirmed TENTATIVE drafts, their pinned post-image bytes and the outbox's claim slots live in daemon memory only: `Outbox.toJson()` omits claim slots, so a restart drops every unemitted draft and every unlanded claim op (a seq allocated to a slot that never landed is a gap, which PRO-08/PRO-09 allow); the restarted daemon has no TENTATIVE claim until the seat's next confirmed edit; B1 still gates every publish — 04 §3.3 ("pinned until that draft is emitted or discarded"), REG-35 ("the draft MUST NOT cause an object to be written"), HLD-36 — cost if wrong: after a restart a teammate may claim a unit this seat was editing; flagged to the 04 owner. (OQ-20, 2Q-18)
- **R4-32 Late-bound claim ops.** Ruling: a draft, or a HELD claim to re-anchor, enters the outbox as a claim slot (`Outbox.addClaimSlot(claimId)`, one per `claim_id`); `computeClaims` (Algorithm EMIT) fills its content against the live code of the board commit being built (C on a code push, TIP on a board-only push) at every build of a publish intent (`Outbox.fillClaim`), before `take()`; the slot's op id is allocated at its first placement and kept across rebuilds (PRO-09) while `anchor`, `base_blob`, `ranges`, `tombstones`, `cs_id` and `label` change; a local-only draft (unit absent from T, or a TOMBSTONE image) stays unfilled and pending without a seq; `Outbox.dropClaim` removes a failed drift re-anchor from the current intent only — PRO-31 ("recomputed for every rebuild of a publish intent"), 04 §3.5 EMIT, REG-22 — cost if wrong: none. (OQ-12, 2Q-18, A-05)
- **R4-33 Recover and the re-anchor mode.** Ruling: in PRO-25's re-anchor mode Plan 2's `lostStateRebuild` drops every claim slot and draft of the old epoch (memory-only anyway, R4-31) and Plan 4 issues no automatic re-claim: the new epoch's `join` folds the old epoch's claims RELEASED (`superseded`) and the seats re-claim through ordinary claim ops at their next confirmed edit — PRO-25 ("ops that name seats or claims of the old epoch are dropped") — cost if wrong: a seat's claims vanish after a recover until its next edit; B1 still gates. (B2-05)
- **R4-34 02 §8 Q6 is superseded.** Ruling: REG-34 (rev 2.1, an owner follow-up) wins over 02 §8 Q6's "no re-derivation" default; listed as a 02 spec edit — newer, owner-decided and cited by F04/F05 — none. (R2-L16)
- **R4-35 Only a new or changed draft starts a claim push.** Ruling: the only claim-initiated push is the governed board-only push that a new or changed draft on a unit present in TIP makes pending (04 §3.5 CLAIM step 4, glossary §27.2: subject to every governor limit including the sliding team guard); a drift re-anchor (REG-11 (b)), a re-anchor caused by an own code change (it rides that commit) and a save release never make the outbox pending on their own; no claim op is emitted on a timer; a rejected claim op is never re-sent unchanged — 02 §3.11 step 2, INV-CONVERGE, INV-PRO-QUIET, PRO-24 — cost if wrong: one extra board-only push. (R2-L17)

**Ports, the gate, placement and merges:**
- **R4-36 Port bindings reach the engine through a host.** Ruling: Plan 3's four ports are constructed at the `EngineDeps` construction sites, before the `EngineCore` exists, so `plan4Ports(host)` takes the `RegionHost` that `productionRegionDeps()` (in tests `testRegionDeps()`) creates and that every `createEngineCore` over those deps attaches to a `ClaimHostView` over the new core (the fold at the last leased tip, the claim store, the registry, `RegionService`, attribution, scope); the host outlives any single core: a later `createEngineCore` over the same deps (Plan 2's `restartCore`, a RESTART in `realEngine`, an upgrade in Task 16) re-attaches, the last core created wins and `get()` returns its view; a port method called before the first attach throws (a construction defect caught by tests); every Plan 3 port signature stays, with only optional trailing arguments and optional result fields added — 04 §3.1 (`gate(…, attribution, claimView@leasedTip)`), R3-03, R3-04, R3-07 port shapes — cost if wrong: a late binding instead of an argument. (OQ-8, 2Q-01, 3Q-03, A-12)
- **R4-37 `PublishGatePort`.** Ruling: `regionGate(host).gate(repo, tip, A, R, paths, csIds)` asserts that `tip` is the host's folded tip, runs Algorithm GATE for that ready group against the claim view there and returns `{block: null}` when nothing is blocked, else `{block: {claimId, claimIds, detail}}` — `claimId` the earliest blocking claim in claim order (R4-29), `claimIds` every blocking claim in that order, `detail` the spec's `{blocked, admitted}` (the last two fields additive); Task 12 edits Plan 3's B1 slot to give the group's one PIN one guard `claim:<id>` per `claimIds` entry, through Plan 3's existing API (R4-74: `hold(pinCs('claim:<claimIds[0]>', …))`, then `addGuard(id, 'claim:<next>')` per further entry, all before the window's push; HLD-07 attaches a repeat block), and to append one `PublishReport.claimPins` entry per PIN it made (`{holdId, kind: 'K3' | 'K6', claimIds, csIds, holders, detail}`, X-28), which Plan 6 reads for its K3/K6 notices and requests; `detail` keeps Plan 4's camelCase form and Plan 6 derives a grant's `req_id` from `ViewClaim.grant` (X-27); `claimStillBlocks(r)` re-runs GATE on `hunks(H0[p], H1[p])` of the record's paths at the host's tip and answers whether any of its `claim:<id>` guards still blocks (true, fail closed, when the host has no fold yet); every call emits the `gate` trace event — 04 §3.7, HLD-45 ("one PIN"; "one request per distinct holder seat", which Plan 6 reads from `detail`), Table 3 row `claim` — cost if wrong: one PIN per claim instead of one per group. (OQ-8, 2X-04, 2X-05, A-12, B3-03)
- **R4-38 `T0RegionsPort`.** Ruling: `check` gains an optional trailing `ctx?: {a: Oid; p: Oid; csIds: readonly string[]}`; the failing verdict's optional `findings` is declared by Plan 3 Task 10 as `ReadonlyArray<{check: 1–4; path; line?}>` (its T0 drop writes `last_check`'s `stale` from `findings[0]`, X-07) and Task 5 narrows its element type to `T0Finding` (`{check: 1|2|3|4; path; line?; reason: EditCheckReason; symbol?: {qualname; simple}}`, assignable to Plan 3's element; numbered by F14 §4's mapping: item-1 error count or UTF-8 → 1, item-1 label count → 4, a non-removal caller or an orphan call → 2, an unresolved import or a removal caller → 3); `regionT0(host).check` runs `regionTripwire(TIP[p], P[p], OUT[p])` (item 1) and `interfaceCheck(A, P, OUT, cs)` (item 2) on the paths with a symbol table and returns `{incomplete: true}` without `ctx` (never a pass); Task 15 edits Plan 3's T0 slot to pass `ctx`; a finding's symbol comes from this daemon's own table (display data for COO-10's `T-WITHHELD_T0`, Plan 6) — HLD-50 items 1–2, 04 §3.10 ("comparing `base[p]` with `ours[p]`"), FEAT-F14-07 ("T0 findings MUST keep their `path:line`") — cost if wrong: an additive field. (OQ-9, 3Q-03, A-02, 2X-03, R2-O01)
- **R4-39 `EditCheckPort`.** Ruling: `check` gains an optional trailing `opts?: {csId?: string; mode?: 'checkpoint' | 'predict'}` (accepted by X-08); the `fail`/`incomplete` verdicts' optional `entries` is declared by Plan 3 Task 10 as `ReadonlyArray<{check?: 1–4; path; line?; reason: string}>` (X-08) and Task 5 narrows its element type to `EditCheckEntry` (`{check?, path, reason, line?}`, R4-42, sorted per R4-08; assignable); `failed` keeps Plan 3's meaning (the distinct check numbers of the entries; Plan 5 reads `entries`, never a re-typed `failed`); Plan 3's `captureStep` keeps reading `verdict`, and its `settleCandidate` writes the `last_check` slot from `entries[0]` (else `failed[0]`) with `more = entries.length − 1` (X-06, X-08); per CS at most one check runs and at most one waits, a newer call replacing the waiting one (which resolves `{verdict: 'incomplete', entries: []}`) and never cancelling the running one; under Plan 3's flow `captureStep` awaits the check inside the iteration, so at most one call per CS is ever in flight there and the queue serves callers outside the iteration (Plan 5's hooks) — Task 10 tests it by calling the port directly — 04 §3.1, §3.20 ("Budget and scheduling"), REG-36 (F14, F12), HLD-67 step 4 — cost if wrong: an additive field. (OQ-10, 2Q-01, A-01, 2X-01, R2-E13)
- **R4-40 Who computes `M`.** Ruling: Plan 3's candidate is materialized (R3-41, 01 §3.8 (a)), so its `A`, `R` and `TIP` blobs are objects, and `regionEditCheck(host).check` computes `M` itself without writing anything: per checked path, `TIP[p] = A[p]` ⇒ `M[p] := R[p]` with no merge (asserted, R4-41); otherwise Plan 2's `mergeFile` (`git merge-file -p --object-id` with MERGE_FLAGS over `A[p]`, `R[p]`, `TIP[p]`, which writes no object) gives `M[p]` as in-memory bytes registered in `SnapshotStore` (oid by `hashRouteC(repo, bytes, false)`), rc > 0 gives `conflicted` (`merge_conflict`), and so does every path both sides changed that `merge-file` cannot take — modify/delete, delete/modify, add/add with different content, a binary or filtered path, modes both changed differently — exactly as `merge-tree` would report it conflicted (R4-73); `not_supplied` (`no_merge`, re-checked at the first checkpoint after the rebasing apply, 04 §3.20) is kept for a merge that could not be computed (an A or TIP blob unreadable after capture, a `merge-file` run that fails with rc ≥ 128); an R blob that cannot be read gives `no_snapshot`; Plan 2's `mergeTree` is not used here because its R-30 wrapper commits and `--write-tree` write objects (REG-35: "No object, index entry or ref is written"); the pure `editCheck` core accepts `DiskSnapshotId`s and in-memory buffers alike (F10, R4-46) — 01 §3.8, ENG-04, 04 §3.20 inputs, §8 Q9 default ("accepts `M[p]` either as a stored blob or as an in-memory handle"), R3-41 — cost if wrong: a content merge per changed path per checkpoint whose TIP moved; spec edits: 04 §3.1 and §3.20 "the `merge-tree --merge-base=A R TIP` result … supplied by 01-engine (ENG-46)" → "the per-path `merge-file` result computed by the edit check from the materialized candidate (01-engine §3.8, ENG-04)"; CCI-56's "MUST write no object for the candidate" is flagged to Plan 5 (the candidate itself is materialized by R3-41; X-36: R3-41 stands and Plan 5's spec edit SE5-03 aligns CCI-56). (2Q-02, 3Q-18, R2-J06)
- **R4-41 The `M[p] = R[p]` assertion over sparse trees.** Ruling: the assertion is per checked path on blob ids; paths outside the CS are read from TIP; a contradiction (a supplied `M[p]` that differs) makes the result `incomplete` and writes one daemon-log line, `EDIT_CHECK_INPUT_MISMATCH {path}` (a defect, never a pass; log-only code, verify pass V-17) — 04 §3.20 "Inputs" bullet 4, Plan 2 R-01 (sparse ready trees) — none. (2Q-23)
- **R4-42 The edit-check reason enum.** Ruling: `EditCheckReason` = `new_error_node | undecodable | conflict_markers` (check 1), `incompatible_caller | orphan_call` (check 2), `unresolved_import | removed_name_called` (check 3), `label_count | shrink | emptied | deleted` (check 4), `no_snapshot | merge_conflict | no_merge` (no `check`); it is display data only (it never changes `pass`) and SEC-38-clean (no names, no code); mode `predict` drops `shrink | emptied | deleted` (D2) — 05 REV step 6, F14 §4, FEAT-F12-06, REG-36; spec edit: 04 §3.20 lists the enum — cost if wrong: a value renamed before Plan 7 renders it. (2Q-09)
- **R4-43 `last_check` belongs to Plan 3.** Ruling (as amended by the 2026-10-08 reconciliation, X-05, X-06): Plan 4 never persists, logs, publishes, traces or writes `last_check` content and creates no store. Plan 3 owns it (roadmap row 3): `src/holds/lastCheck.ts` (`LastCheckKind`, `SkipReason`, `LastCheck {edit_n; kind; check?; path?; line?; skip?; more}` — glossary §29.8's shape exactly — and `LastCheckStore {record, get, pass, clearCs, noteCandidate, recordFor, passedFor, sharedFor}`), one memory-only instance `ChangeSets.lastCheck`, created by Plan 3's Task 10 amendment; Plan 3's `settleCandidate` writes HLD-67 step 4's `fail`/`conflict`/`incomplete` from the verdict's `entries[0]` (else `failed[0]`) with `more = entries.length − 1`, Plan 3's T0 slot writes `stale` from `T0Verdict.findings[0]`, and Plan 5's `editCheckpoints.ts` writes `skipped` through `recordFor`. Plan 4's whole share is the verdict: Task 10's `regionEditCheck` returns every failed entry in `entries` and Task 9's `regionT0` returns `findings` — roadmap row 3 (rev 2.5, F14/F12), QUEUE Q5, X-05, X-06, X-08 — cost if wrong: none for Plan 4 (it touches no store). (2Q-03, 2X-08)
- **R4-44 Pathspecs of the region greps.** Ruling: the caller grep, the edit check's split grep and the partial-type grep run with `GitCallOptions.literalPathspecs` unset and spell every path with explicit magic — `:(literal)<p>` in the `-- <paths with a symbol table>` list, `:(exclude,literal)<p>` for the edit check's excluded checked paths — so paths containing `*`, `?`, `[` or a leading `:` stay literal (a unit test greps `a*[b].py` and `:x.py`); no `RunOpts.config` is passed: `core.quotepath` is one of Plan 2's forced keys (a forced key passed through `config` throws), and the runner already emits `-c core.quotepath=off` on every call — 04 §3.10, §3.20; Plan 2's `GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS` disables all magic — cost if wrong: a glob-named path is mis-grepped (caught by T-REG-28's property). (OQ-26, R2-E12)
- **R4-45 The partial-type grep on the private index.** Ruling: keep 04's grep (`git grep -z -n -E 'partial[[:space:]]+(class|struct|record|interface)[[:space:]]+<Name>\b' --cached` with `GIT_INDEX_FILE` = the private index, 100 ms) and its whole-file fallback; Plan 2's private index names only written blobs (detection ticks refresh stat data on scratch indexes; only `materialize` writes entries, after their blobs), so 04 §8 Q8 is listed closed; a unit test plants an index entry naming a missing object and asserts the fallback (never "no conflict"); a failed or timed-out grep returns `{failed: true, files: []}` — `TableCache` keeps no index of partial declarations, so the fallback relies on `groupNameHit`'s `hunk.fileDeclaresPartial` (the hunk's own post bytes through `declaresPartial`), which catches every hunk in a file that declares the partial type — REG-17, glossary §26.1 W6, Plan 2 `Snapshotter` — cost if wrong: over-blocking on partial types (P2). (2Q-12, 3Q-18)
- **R4-46 F10 predictions.** Ruling: `predictRevert(host, results, tip)` (Task 12; positional, as §Interfaces declares it — verify pass V-16) runs on the in-memory result buffers of `revertInverse()`, with `TIP` elsewhere and `A = TIP` on the result paths (BUSY): (a) `gate` with every result hunk attributed to this peer's `human` (so claims of this PC's own seats are not foreign, REG-19) → `wouldHold: GateBlocked[]`, and (b) the edit check in mode `predict` with `M[p]` = the result buffer → `wouldBreak: EditCheckEntry[]`; the buffers are registered in `SnapshotStore` under an owner token released when the call returns, their oids computed by Plan 2's `hashRouteC(repo, bytes, false)` (no write); the prediction writes no object, record, `last_check`, capture or trace event; the verb and the `UI_REVERT_WOULD_*` lines are Plan 7's, the kernel Plan 3's (Task 17, `src/holds/revertCs.ts`, X-09: Plan 7's revert verb passes `revertInverse()`'s `results` to `Engine.predictRevert` and renders `GateBlocked[]` with `claimLabelText(verifiedLabel(…))` and `EditCheckEntry[]`, X-35) — 05 REV step 6, REG-35 and REG-36 (F10 clauses), INV-REG-EDITCHECK, D2 — cost if wrong: a display line. (R1-127, R2-E15, F-03, 2X-02)
- **R4-47 Conflict-function hooks.** Ruling: `ConflictCtx` gains optional members — `hasGrammar?(path)` (D9 (b)), `need?(path, L)` (D9 step 3: 2 when L's first non-blank row starts a top-level Python definition unit, 1 for a nested Python or a C#/JS/TS definition unit, else 0, from `regionTable(s2)`), `tripwire?(s3, s2, out)` (D9 step 5 leg (ii) and D8 step 0a: `regionTripwire` forms (a)/(b) within the 400 ms parse budget; a violation or a timeout means "not qualified") and `tieBreak?(path, s1, s2, s3)` (D8 step 4: `touch` on the conflict rows against the claim view at the lease value → `'breach'` when the LOCAL holder's claim on those rows is earlier in claim order than every TIP-side claim, else `'k4'`); `ConflictVerdict`'s `TAKE_TIP` gains an optional `breach: true`, and the caller adds guard `claim-breach` (Plan 3 `makeGuard`) to the K4 record; an absent member keeps Plan 2's behaviour exactly; Task 9 also fixes Plan 2's ledger minor at `conflict.ts:234` (D9 step 3's blank-row ending uses the base row before the region, LF if none) — 03 D8 steps 0a and 4, D9, DAT-42, DAT-44, Plan 2 R-39 — cost if wrong: none. (OQ-22, 2Q-08, 2X-18, B2-06, B2-07)
- **R4-48 Placement call sites.** Ruling: the engine calls `placementCheck` directly (Task 15), not through a port: in `applyStep` on every path where `mergeTree` returned rc 0 and both sides changed it (sub-check (a)), and in `publishStep` after OUT and before T0 on every both-sides path ((a)) and every path where OUT ≠ TIP with a foreign claim ((b), against this window's B1 results); a failed path enters `conflict()` as a `'content'` unit and gets `TAKE_TIP` (TIP on disk and in OUT, the local blob a CONFLICTED record, K4), never a group PIN; the check reuses the table-cache entries T0 parses — ENG-29, DAT-41, 04 §3.8 — cost if wrong: none. (OQ-23, B2-08)
- **R4-49 D8 step 4 gets its own test.** Ruling: 03 names none; Task 15 adds it: a holder's unpublished edit inside its own HELD claim, then a teammate's commit changing the same rows lands → TIP on disk and in OUT, one CONFLICTED record with guards `conflict` and `claim-breach`, the path pinned OFF_DISK (O-OFFDISK's exception), and one hit in the breach sink — 03 §6 ("TIP breached the holder's earlier claim"), DAT-44 — none. (3Q-14)

(OQ-35, A-06, 2X-06, B3-04)
- **R4-51 The breach sink.** Ruling: `breach` hits go to `BreachPort.onBreach(hits)` once per apply — the only route breaches travel (X-28: no `ApplyReport` field) — and each `BreachHit` carries `holdId: string | null`, the id of the K4 record this apply gave guard `claim-breach` on that path (D8 step 4), else null (X-28); until Plan 6 binds COO-08 (one `event{kind: breach}` and `T-BREACH`) it is bound to `logBreach`, which writes one daemon-log line per hit (ids only) and emits the `breach` trace event — COO-08, INV-ONE-EVENT — cost if wrong: none. **Labels, codes and status:**

(OQ-34, 3Q-10, 2X-27)

Reconciliation (X-117, X-30): `src/regions/codes.ts` is the one module that declares Plan 4's codes — the eight above, `START_REGION_RUNTIME {file}` (Task 16's start refusal; its text, registry row and LIF-36 handling are Plan 7's) and `REGION_LOG_ONLY_CODES = ['FOLD_OBJECT_MISSING', 'EDIT_CHECK_OVERRUN', 'REGION_OBJECT_MISSING', 'EDIT_CHECK_INPUT_MISMATCH', 'REGION_WORKER_RESPAWN', 'REGION_RUNTIME_MISMATCH', 'CLAIM_BREACH_HIT']` (the two daemon-log records of R4-76 and Task 10, renamed from lowercase, and the five other daemon-log lines named by the verify pass, V-17); Plan 7's `carried.ts` imports the module (no retyping) and registers the nine message codes and the seven log-only codes (`surfaces: ['log']`); L-CODES fails a duplicate declaration and an unregistered `Log.write` code — 04 §3.19, PRO-24, PRO-38, R-27 — cost if wrong: a status line. (OQ-29, R2-D01, R2-D02, B2-12)

- **R4-59 The exit profile `claims`.** Ruling: 3 peers (odd peers `core.autocrlf=true`, peer 2 with `platform: 'win32'` on every OS as R3-47), 2 scripted seats on each of peers 0 and 1, peer 2 human only, 60 generated steps per seed; seed files: a 600-line BOM+CRLF Python file, LF and CRLF TypeScript and C# files (one C# partial type split over two files), a 250-line and a 900-line Markdown file and a cp949 C# file over 300 lines; ops: scripted-seat edits through `Engine.seatEffect` (body edits, appends at a unit's last line, decorator and comment insertions above a unit, new units, `rename_unit`, `move_unit` within and across files, deletions), the same edits as hook-free writes, same-function races between two seats of one peer and of two peers, human edits into claimed units from the owning PC and from another PC, `same_tail_append`, half-typed boundary insertions, peer churn above `D_reanchor`, seat ends; oracles: INV-FENCE, O-REG-MAP, O-REG-FOLD, O-REG-TRIPWIRE, O-REG-NOLABEL, O-REG-COORD, the INV-REG-READ and INV-REG-NOWRITE checks (R4-61), Plan 2's six and Plan 3's three exit oracles and its 05 rows; exit: 1,000 seeds per OS; PR legs: 15 seeds per leg (X-113; was 20) — roadmap row 4 exit (which names no seed count), R-31, R-33, R3-47, R3-51 — cost if wrong: SIM time, measured and sized in Task 18 by R3-55's rule. []`); no event carries a duration, a wall time, a label, a byte offset or an entry's `reason`/`line` (D1) — TST-03, 11 §3.4 O-EDITCHECK, INV-REG-COORD — cost if wrong: none. (B2-14, A-03, 2X-07)

- **R4-66 F04, F05 and F12 shares.** Ruling: **F04** — Task 13 adds the claim rows (a new claim, a re-anchor, each release reason, a verified and an unverified label) and the T-F04-golden "label not verified → omitted" variant to `src/since/report.ts`'s fixtures after F04-a has merged (X-76: F04-a is the Plan 2 controller's follow-up F-P2-1, `src/since/report.ts`, planned in `p2-engine/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-cowork-sync-f04a-since.md`; its label slot is `sinceInit(start, from, you, mine, o?: {verifiedLabel?: (claimId, anchor) => SinceLabel | null})`, passed through by `walkSince(repo, b, o)` — there is no function named `sinceReport` (verify pass V-6, X-23); `SinceLabel` is structural, Plan 4's `ClaimLabel` is assignable and Task 13 may retype the slot to `ClaimLabel` in place); if F04-a has not merged when Task 13 runs, the three `[F04-a]` tests run in a Plan 4 follow-up task when it merges (ledger entry, owner: the Plan 4 controller); the lookup never schedules a parse; **F05** — no task: Task 11's `claimViewOf(seat)` (sibling drafts with their pinned `srcBlob`, never unconfirmed ones), Task 3's `mapRanges(…, 'local')` over a `DiskSnapshotId` target and Task 13's `verifiedLabel` are its prerequisites, each unit-tested; **F12** — Task 13 exports `labelsOfChange` (the `RegionMapper` that LIF-27 and F12's `attributionRows` consume: labels of the leaf units touched by `hunks(c^1[p], c[p])`, a whole-file label for `NO_TABLE` paths, `save` priority) — QUEUE Q6, roadmap row 4, FEAT-F04-06, FEAT-F05-01, X-76 — cost if wrong: the F04 fixture rows land in the Plan 4 follow-up task when F04-a merges. (R1-160…R1-164, R2-O04, R2-O05, F-04…F-06, A-07, A-08)
- **R4-67 05 P5 runs here.** Ruling: Task 17 runs 05 P5 ("exp_a and exp_bc with claims on, off and racing randomly give identical S_pub and record sequences") on Task 2's regenerated fixtures, and Task 5's structural test keeps Plan 3's "the revert detector, MASK, REBASE, PARK, RESTORE and R1 modules import nothing that reads claim state" green with `src/claims` added to the forbidden set — HLD-06, INV-HLD-CLAIMFREE, Plan 3 Task 9's note, R3-49 — none. (B3-06)
- **R4-68 Plan 8a rows.** Ruling (as amended by the 2026-10-08 reconciliation, X-91, and by the owner's choice of 2026-10-08 on X-92): Plan 4 owns its viewer rows; they land in follow-up task **4-V** (after Task 18), run when 8a Task 20 (the viewer's claim inputs, which needs Task 8's `overlapPairsOf`) has merged — never in Task 8's commit; goldens regenerate only through 8a Task 16's `npm run viz:golden -- --update` (a prerequisite, F-8a-5). The rows: a `claim` with a new `claim_id` → `claim_take`; a re-anchor → `claim_move`; a `release` or a fold-derived release (a new-epoch `join` → `superseded`, `leave` → `stop`, `seat_end`, `save`) → `claim_release{reason}`, at their own positions between checkpoints; a claim op that folds invalid → no event (verdict `rejected`, counted in `unknown`); `startScene` reads the `claims` fields Plan 4 fills (chairs, ghost chairs for STALE, labels marked unverified, 12 §8 Q6 default; `ClaimState.at` per R-V73); checkpoints compare plainly (no re-seed, no exempt member); a `hold` PIN with guard `claim` or `claim:<id>` → `conflict{claim}` + `box`; a `claim-breach` record → its 12 §3.4 event; 8a's Plan-4-marked "unfolded in Plan 2" fixtures are re-marked to these outcomes (V-01); T-VIZ-golden-sim passes after a reviewed `--update`; `claim_stale`, grants and reservations (`grant_*`, `claim_reserve`) are Plan 6's rows — roadmap "Execution order", 12 §3.4, X-91, X-92, OWNER-CHOICES item 1 — cost if wrong: the rows wait for 8a Task 20. (R1-154, R2-O06, V-01, V-02)

**Review round 1 (2026-10-08; revision log `.superpowers/sdd/draft/revision-log.md`):**
- **R4-69 X7 is one fixture.** Ruling: X7 is Task 2's `interface/X7/{base,ours,theirs}`: `lib.py` `def foo(a, b)` becomes `def foo(a)` on one side (ours, the CS that changed the signature) while the other side (theirs) adds `orders.py` row 14 `    total = foo(price, qty)`, so the CAS re-merge OUT publishes a 2-argument caller at `orders.py:14` (`Breaking` kind `param_removed`, check 2, K7 item 2); X7's `orders.py` is its own file, not the 5-row PRICE `orders.py` of Task 7's Notes; the "`compute_price` gains a required `currency`" scenario stays x2's E1 under that name (Tasks 10 and 12) — 04 §3.10 VERIFIED X7 ("a 2-argument caller … `orders.py:14`"), T-REG-09 ("`foo(a,b)→foo(a)`"), T-REG-28 rev 2.5 ("the un-updated definite caller of `foo` sits in a file outside the CS … its TIP row as `line`") — cost if wrong: one fixture renamed; every X7 expectation of Tasks 2, 7, 9, 10, 13 and 15 follows this definition.
- **R4-70 TC-REG-09 is grouped by REG-36's check numbers.** Ruling: the edit-check corpus numbers its fixtures as REG-36, F14 §4, `interfaceEntries`, `t0CheckNumber` and R4-38 do: check 2 = a non-removal breaking change (`param_removed`, `param_reordered`, `param_renamed`, `required_added`, `varargs_removed`, C# `overload_gone`) with an un-updated definite caller, the orphan call, and the TIP-side caller not updated; check 3 = a removed or renamed symbol still called, a removed export still imported, an import of a name the merged tree lacks; every fail fixture reports exactly one check number (`failed` deep-equals `[n]`), so `M46_n` lets exactly the check-n fail fixtures pass; spec edit 13 — REG-36 ("`incompatibleCallers` of removals → 3"), F14 §4; 11 TC-REG-09 (2) and glossary §27.1 item 2 list removals under check 2 — cost if wrong: two fixture rows move between check groups.
- **R4-71 The 15 ms gate budget is `predict`'s.** Ruling: glossary §20 "Gate latency" (04 §3.18 row 1: daemon side, from cache, ≤ 15 ms p95) budgets the PreToolUse region decision: `predict` runs under `budgets.deadline('gate')` and on expiry answers `status: 'undecided'` (G2, CONFIRM decides; never "no conflict") and raises `REGION_BUDGET {path, budget: 'gate'}` through `RegionServiceDeps.onBudget`; the B1 publish gate (`gate`, Algorithm GATE) has no time budget in the spec and runs to completion (a slow B1 delays a publish, never passes one); Task 18 times `predict` for T-REG-15's gate row and reports B1's p95 as an extra field only — 04 §3.11 ("answered from memory within the gate budget"), §3.18 rows 1–2, glossary §26.2 item 4, REG-31 — cost if wrong: a timing row moves between two functions; expected effect, not a defect: a C# boundary case whose group-name step needs `partialTypeFiles` (a 100 ms grep) cannot finish in 15 ms, so it answers `undecided` (G2) and raises `REGION_BUDGET {budget: 'gate'}` at most once per path per 30 min — 04 §3.18 row 2's own degradation path.
- **R4-72 The caller grep has its own 4× stop.** Ruling: `interfaceCheck` runs its caller search (part (i) `git grep`, part (ii) and the hit filtering) under the earlier of the caller's deadline and `budgets.deadline('caller_grep', 4)` (600 ms, 04 §3.18 "`incomplete` past 4×"); `InterfaceResult.incompleteBudget` (`'grep' | 'caller' | null`) says which deadline stopped it, so `regionT0` raises `REGION_BUDGET {path, budget: 'grep'}` or `{path, budget: 't0'}` (REG-31), while the edit check raises neither (REG-31's exception) — 04 §3.10 "Budget", §3.18 "Caller grep + filtering", §3.19's `grep` value — cost if wrong: a slow grep inside T0 reports incomplete one loop earlier.
- **R4-73 Unmergeable both-sides paths are `merge_conflict`.** Ruling: `regionEditCheck` gives `{kind: 'conflicted'}` (reason `merge_conflict`) for every path both sides changed that `merge-file` cannot take (modify/delete, delete/modify, add/add with different content, a binary or filtered path, modes both changed differently), as `merge-tree --merge-base=A R TIP` (04 §3.20's `M[p]`) would report it, and keeps `not_supplied` (`no_merge`) for a merge it could not compute (an A or TIP blob unreadable after capture, a `merge-file` run ending rc ≥ 128); `R[p] = TIP[p]` gives `M[p] = R[p]` without a merge; HLD-67 step 4 / F14 §4 — Plan 3's `settleCandidate`, which writes the slot (X-06) — then record `last_check{kind: 'conflict'}`, not `incomplete`, for them — 04 §3.20 inputs, F14 §4, FEAT-F12-06 — cost if wrong: a display kind; the verdict (fail) is the same.
- **R4-74 Several claim guards on one PIN use Plan 3's API.** Ruling: `NewRecord.guard` and `pinCs(guard, …)` take one guard and `commitMany` patches only existing ids, so Task 12's B1 slot creates the group's one PIN with `hold(pinCs('claim:<claimIds[0]>', …))` and adds every further claim with `addGuard(id, 'claim:<next>')`, all in the same `publishStep` before the window's push (one registry commit per guard); no Plan 3 contract change — Plan 3's `NewRecord`, `pinCs`, `commitMany`, `addGuard`; HLD-45 ("one PIN"), HLD-07 — cost if wrong: a crash between `hold` and an `addGuard` leaves the PIN with fewer guards; it still holds the whole group (fail closed) and the next B1 call on the group attaches the missing block through HLD-07.
- **R4-75 (withdrawn by the 2026-10-08 reconciliation, X-05, X-06).** The conditional store this ruling created (`src/holds/lastCheck.ts`, `EngineCore.lastCheck`, Task 5) is not created: Plan 3 Task 10 owns the store as `ChangeSets.lastCheck` and writes it (R4-43 as amended); Task 5 adds no `EngineCore.lastCheck` member and Task 10 writes no slot, so Wave D's Tasks 8 and 10 still never edit `core.ts` together.
- **R4-76 A missing fold object ends the iteration, not the loop.** Ruling (as amended by the 2026-10-08 reconciliation, X-30, X-31): Task 8 (which already edits `src/engine/core.ts`) makes `prepareIteration` catch `FoldObjectMissingError` from `Folder.foldTo` and return `{ok: false, why: 'fold_object_missing', oid}` (Plan 2's `Prepared` gains the value; Table A row A-9), leaving `int.fold` and `core.leasedFold` unchanged; that iteration applies and publishes nothing and the next one's fetch retries. Plan 2's landed `iterate()` has no exhaustive switch on `Prepared.why` (it logs `ITERATION_SKIPPED {why}` for every non-incident skip, `p2-t17/src/engine/loop.ts:503–507`), so no `why` case is added there. A miss that persists is surfaced in the shape of Plan 2's Task 14 `push_unresolved` carry (X-31): `prepareIteration` keeps the first miss's anchor in memory on the core (`core.foldMissing: {oid; sinceMono; loggedMono} | null`, cleared by the next fold that completes), and once the miss is older than 60 s Δmono the status inputs carry `not_converged` cause `fold_object_missing` (state `degraded`; `NotConvergedCause` in `src/engine/status.ts` gains the value) and the daemon log gets `FOLD_OBJECT_MISSING {oid}` at most once per 10 min — a registry code with `surfaces: ['log']`, declared in `src/regions/codes.ts` (X-30, renamed from the lowercase `fold_object_missing` record of controller flag 16); no LOST_STATE escalation in v1 (the question is the 02 owner's) — Plan 2's `prepareIteration` rethrows every error it does not map, which would end `iterate()`; 04 §6 ("the fold does not guess") — cost if wrong: a peer idles, `degraded`, until the object arrives with a later fetch.
- **R4-77 Per-peer port bindings in the fixtures.** Ruling (as amended by the 2026-10-08 reconciliation, X-15): Plan 3 Task 10 declares `CoreOptions.ports?: PortsOverride | ((deps: EngineDeps, peerIndex: number) => PortsOverride)` (the same option on `makeEngines`; `PortsOverride` = `Partial<Pick<EngineDeps, 'editCheck' | 't0Regions' | 'gate' | 'symbols'>>`) — Plan 3 cannot name Plan 4's `RegionHost` — and calls the function once per peer with that peer's deps; Plan 4's bindings read the host from them (`(deps) => ({gate: regionGate(deps.regions.host)})`); the static object form keeps Plan 3's meaning; Task 5 only makes both fixtures set `deps.regions` before the call (it already adds `regions: RegionDeps` at the construction sites); Table A row A-17 — a static object is built before any peer's host exists, and one shared binding would attach to the wrong core — cost if wrong: a test-helper signature.
- **R4-78 The worker pool's lifecycle lands with the pool.** Ruling: Task 5's `src/daemon/run.ts` change, which first spawns the production pool through `productionRegionDeps`, also awaits `regions.workers.close()` after drain and on every early exit; Task 16 keeps only the `START_REGION_RUNTIME` refusal and the regionSpec assertion — live `worker_threads` keep the `dist/daemon.js` process alive, so Plan 2's daemon CLI tests (SIGINT, then wait for exit) would hang from Task 5 to Task 15 — cost if wrong: none.
- **R4-79 T0 findings travel on the port's verdict only.** Ruling: Plan 4 adds no carrier for T0 findings: `regionT0(host).check` returns them in `T0Verdict.findings`, Plan 3's T0 slot holds that verdict where it makes the K7 PIN, and their consumers there — `last_check`'s `stale` slot, which Plan 3 Task 10's T0 drop writes from `findings[0]` (X-06, X-07), and `T-WITHHELD_T0` (Plan 6) — read them from it (Table C C-19); Task 15 asserts them through a recording wrapper around the bound port — `HoldRecord` has no findings field and a record is not a display surface (D1 keeps display data off records) — cost if wrong: Plan 6 adds a memory map in the T0 slot.
- **R4-80 TC-REG-01's tripwire half is asserted per scenario.** Ruling: Task 2's manifest records, per corrupting scenario, the mechanism that catches its regenerated hazard (`caughtBy`: `tripwire_b`, `tripwire_c`, `b1`, `placement` or `t0`); Task 7 asserts form (b) only for the same-file duplicated unit keys (S2a, S3a, S3b, S3c, S6b) and form (c) for S8; S2b (a cross-file duplicate), S4 (deleted claimed rows), S5a/S5b (a duplicated decorator row inside one unit), S6a and S9 (a class-6 block table) are asserted held through their mechanism by Task 15's region-gap suite — 04 §3.15's "every corrupt splice in S2–S9 parsed clean; the count rule (b) flagged them" describes the lost research corpus (R4-21), and T-REG-01 accepts any mechanism ("must all be held") — cost if wrong: a scenario the research flagged by (b) is caught here by B1, placement or T0, still before any push.
- **R4-81 The production regionSpec assertion is a pure check.** Ruling: `assertRuntimeRegionSpec(engineRegionSpec, runtimeRegionSpec)` (exported from `src/daemon/run.ts`) throws `RegionSpecMismatchError` on inequality; `runDaemon` calls it before `Engine.create` unless its git runner is a test build (Plan 1's `testBuild` flag, the switch that already gates `wallMsForDates`) and maps the error to exit 2 with one daemon-log line; Task 16 tests the function directly, the guard by a source check and the test-build path through `makeEngines` — an in-process test cannot be a release build while Plan 7's L-DIST strips the seam — cost if wrong: the exit-2 mapping is pinned by a source check instead of a run.
- **R4-82 T-REG-29's SIM clauses.** Ruling: Task 17's scripted SIM seats gain `checkpoint(paths)` (Plan 3's `CsSignal` `checkpoint{source: 'edit', candSeq, paths}`, `candSeq` counting from 1 per seat CS) and run all three T-REG-29 SIM clauses with 4 peers and B = 12: forced-fail byte identity; ten passing mid-turn checkpoints publishing before turn end (receiver-apply latency reported, never gated here); a turn whose every edit leaves a definite caller un-updated, publishing nothing mid-turn and ending in exactly one K7 PIN at the close; the publish p95 ≤ 40 s soak gate is Plan 7's (TST-17) and the Claude-seat forms Plan 5's (Table C C-18) — 04 T-REG-29, R4-61 — cost if wrong: SIM time.
- **R4-83 Lint and scan scopes.** Ruling: L-UTF8-WRITE's `.write(<string>` clause fails only when the receiver's last identifier is neither `log` nor ends in `Log` (Plan 2's `Log.write(level, code, params)` and the security log write codes, not encoded text); L-ONE-MAPPER's `src/holds/mask.ts` clause is added by Task 5 in the commit that switches `mask.ts` to `hunks()` (Tasks 1–4 rebase onto merged Plan 3, whose `mask.ts` still imports `matchedRows`); Task 5's code-collision scan checks definitions, not uses, and (reconciliation X-29, one rule for all plans, X-117) fails on an exact duplicate code name declared in two `src/**/codes.ts` modules — never on a prefix: Plan 6's `CLAIM_BREACH` and `CLAIM_BREACH_SELF` stay in `src/coord/codes.ts` — 04 §3.2 item 3, R4-17, R4-54 — cost if wrong: a lint row reworded.

**C. What Plan 4 assumes from later or parallel plans (first derived from the spec; every row resolved by the 2026-10-08 reconciliation, ruling ids in the row; Plan 4's stub until the owner's code lands):**

Plan 5 calls `seatEffect` at PostToolUse of Edit/Write/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit and at TXN close (CCI-25, CCI-27) with `pre` = the fence/TXN pre-image blobRef and `post` = a `DiskSnapshotId` from one stable read or `'unstable'`, and again for the same `csId` at the seat's next stable read while the result is `deferred`; emits `seat_start` no later than the seat's first claim (PRO-20) and `seat_end` plus `release{seat_end}` (CCI-32) | test-only scripted seats (`test/helpers/seats.ts`, Task 11; `test/sim/seats.ts`, Task 17) |

Off the gate's reply path (PostToolUse confirmation, TXN close, hook reads) `oid` is Plan 2's route (b) without `-w` (`hashRouteB` of a `stagedRead` copy, or Plan 5's `hashRouteBStdin` of its short-read bytes — the same route, R5-26). **On the gate's reply path** (PreToolUse, CCI-12: no git process) `oid` is Plan 5's in-process `blobOidOfBytes(bytes, objectFormat)` — git's blob hash of the raw short-read bytes, no filter applied; it identifies those working bytes for the region cache and equals the route-(b) oid whenever no filter or eol conversion applies, so a mismatch only costs a parse, never a false "same content". Plan 4 treats a `DiskSnapshotId.oid` only as a content key (never as the stored form of `A[p]`). Releases its owner token at fence release and CS close | tests build handles with `testSnapshot(store, path, bytes, owner?)` |
| C-5 | Plan 5 (X-20) | **Resolved.** Calls `RegionsPort.predict` (= `Engine.predict`) at PreToolUse (gate rows 3, 5, 9, 10, 12, 13, 13b) with the view read at Pre (no `atHeight`); the predicted TENTATIVE claims (`recordTentative`/`discardTentative`, discarded at fence release) and `foreignHeldOnPath` (derived from `claimViewOf`) are Plan 5's own fence and lease state, never Plan 4's `ClaimStore` (which holds only confirmed drafts, R4-04) | unit tests over recorded hook payloads |
| C-6 | Plan 5 | **Resolved (verify pass V-8): no separate Plan 5 binding in v1.** Plan 5's CCI-27 windows (`AttributionWindows`) attribute each live delta to a seat at write time and so decide which CS (`<seat_id>/<turn-seq>` or `<peer_id>/human/<n>`) the delta lands in; Plan 5 keeps no per-CS or per-hunk history after the window closes. `csAttribution` over the group's `cs_id`s is therefore CCI-27's table at path granularity (REG-19), and it stays the `HunkAttributionPort` binding; a hunk of a path two CSs of one group touched stays attributed to both (R4-05's safe direction). Per-hunk refinement would need a Plan 5 history and is not planned | `csAttribution` (R4-05) |

## File Structure

```
package.json                        (modify) devDependencies web-tree-sitter (exact 0.27.x) and the four grammar packages (exact); scripts sim:exit:claims, test:perf:regions
tools/build/build.mjs               (modify) bundles src/entry/worker.ts with web-tree-sitter into dist/worker.js; copies tree-sitter.wasm and the five grammar .wasm into dist/grammars/
tools/lint/rules.ts                 (modify) L-REGION-DEPS, L-REGION-WRITE, L-OFFSETS, L-UTF8-WRITE, L-ONE-MAPPER, L-CLAIMFREE-HOLDS
src/entry/worker.ts                 (modify) the region worker entry: runs src/regions/worker/main.ts (was Plan 1's --version stub); dispatches each job by its kind and rejects an unknown kind, so Plan 7 can append `secret_scan` (X-37)
src/regions/types.ts                shared region types: BlobRef, DiskSnapshotId, Range(s), Hunk, MapResult, Label, LabelKey, Granularity, Unit, RegionTable, NoTable, PathClass, TreeView
src/regions/lines.ts                line arrays (0x0A only, Plan 2 rows), strict read-only decode, blank rows, closed-interval range helpers (REG-01, R4-18)
src/regions/mapper.ts               FoldMapper/1: line equality, hunks, D, mapRanges in fold/local mode, images (REG-07)
src/regions/runtime.ts              languages, extensions, GRAMMAR_VERSIONS, loadRegionRuntime, computeRegionSpec (REG-03, REG-04)
src/regions/queries/{python,javascript,typescript,tsx,c_sharp}.ts   cowork's query sources (string constants, R4-11)
src/regions/queries/index.ts        QUERY_SOURCES (one per language)
src/regions/budgets.ts              04 §3.18 numbers, Deadline, RegionBudgets on the Clock port, BudgetInjector (test builds)
src/regions/pool.ts                 RegionWorkers port, the RegionJob union, threadWorkers (the only worker_threads importer in src/regions)
src/regions/codes.ts                the seven 04 §3.19 codes, CLAIM_REJECTED, their params; START_REGION_RUNTIME; REGION_LOG_ONLY_CODES (FOLD_OBJECT_MISSING, EDIT_CHECK_OVERRUN, REGION_OBJECT_MISSING, EDIT_CHECK_INPUT_MISMATCH, REGION_WORKER_RESPAWN, REGION_RUNTIME_MISMATCH, CLAIM_BREACH_HIT; V-17) — Plan 4's one codes module (X-117, X-30)
src/regions/pathClass.ts            04 §3.3 path classes 1–6 and the language decision
src/regions/snapshots.ts            SnapshotStore: in-memory bytes of DiskSnapshotIds and F10 buffers, owner tokens (REG-35)
src/regions/cache.ts                TableCache: 300 MB LRU by blobRef, pins, last-good index by path
src/regions/service.ts              RegionService: blob and table reads (read-only git), hunks and mapRanges over blobRefs, availability
src/regions/touch.ts                the one touch predicate (REG-13)
src/regions/contained.ts            the containment form (REG-33)
src/regions/groupName.ts            group-name hits for C# and TypeScript (REG-17), pure
src/regions/partial.ts              the partial-type git grep on the private index and its fallback (REG-17)
src/regions/tripwire.ts             relative region tripwires (a)–(c) (REG-24)
src/regions/interface.ts            signature-slice classes, arity rules, finding types, the stoplist (REG-26, REG-27), pure
src/regions/interfaceService.ts     interfaceCheck: caller/callee/import searches over a TreeView (git grep split, module resolution), deadlines
src/regions/editCheck.ts            the four checks and the failed-entry assembly with `line` (REG-36, F14, F12), pure over tables and an interface result
src/regions/editCheckService.ts     editCheck orchestration over M', budget 1×
src/regions/foldOverlap.ts          04 §3.9 foldOverlap, pure
src/regions/foldBatch.ts            foldOverlapBatch: one worker job per board commit
src/regions/worker/protocol.ts      WorkerInit, ToWorker, FromWorker, WorkerPortLike (types only)
src/regions/worker/main.ts          the worker message loop (job dispatch, deadlines)
src/regions/worker/parser.ts        web-tree-sitter init from bytes, per-language load status, parse with deadline, ≤ 8 live trees
src/regions/worker/lineTable.ts     the one tree-sitter row helper (L-OFFSETS)
src/regions/worker/conventions.ts   extent-convention/1, label-convention/1
src/regions/worker/tables.ts        buildTable (units, residue, gap blocks, <imports>, error spans, label counts), blockTable
src/regions/worker/slices.ts        sigSlice extraction; call, import and identifier sites for the interface check
src/regions/worker/jobs.ts          the job dispatcher (Task 1), one import per handler
src/regions/worker/jobs/{hunks,map,table,foldOverlap,sites,labelAt}.ts   one handler file per RegionJob kind (Task 1 stubs; Tasks 3, 3, 4, 8, 7, 13 fill them)
src/regions/worker/inline.ts        inlineWorkers (unit tests and SIM only; never imported by src/daemon or src/engine)
src/board/ops.ts                    (modify) ClaimOp, ReleaseOp (R4-25)
src/board/fold.ts                   (modify) ClaimRec, claim validity and effects, claimFoldNeeds, inputs, grants/reservations helpers, claim order, rejections (R4-26…R4-29)
src/board/outbox.ts                 (modify) claim slots, the record-creating withhold (R4-30…R4-32)
src/sec/validators.ts               (modify) validId('claim'), validLabel
src/ports/trace.ts                  (modify) TraceEvent + gate, claim, placement, breach; edit_check.checks? (R4-63)
src/data/attrs.ts                   (modify) working-tree-encoding
src/data/conflict.ts                (modify) the D9 grammar legs, D8 step 0a tripwire, the D8 step-4 tie-break hook, the :234 fix
src/holds/ports.ts                  (modify) widened port types (R4-37…R4-39, R4-50)
src/holds/mask.ts                   (modify) held ranges from hunks() (R4-17)
src/claims/types.ts                 SeatKey, ViewClaim, ClaimView, Draft, SeatEffect, ConfirmResult, PredictQuery, PredictResult, Gate*, Breach*
src/claims/host.ts                  RegionHost, ClaimHostView (R4-36)
src/claims/store.ts                 ClaimStore: drafts in local order, pinned post-images, deferred confirms, blocked CSs
src/claims/view.ts                  the claim view, claimViewOf(seat), claims mapped into a base (M22 site)
src/claims/overlapPairs.ts          overlapPairsOf: the pure needs → OverlapClaim pairing, tombstone rule included, shared by the daemon and the viewer; types-only imports (R4-26, R4-27)
src/claims/foldInputs.ts            claimFoldResolver: tree entries, blob line arrays, foldOverlap answers for the fold (pairs through overlapPairsOf)
src/claims/confirm.ts               CONFIRM and Algorithm CLAIM (REG-15, REG-18, REG-21, REG-29)
src/claims/predict.ts               PREDICT and the fallback region (REG-28)
src/claims/emit.ts                  Algorithm EMIT, re-anchors, tombstones, claimPrecheck, saveReleaseDue (REG-09…REG-11, REG-22)
```
